Last week it finally got me written up at work, so I really need to figure out how to make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes tend to happen in the same situations each time, like when you’re stressed or being watched?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed a specific trigger, to be honest. I mean, ringing up customers can be a little stressful sometimes, especially when there's a long line, but it doesn't only happen then. And I don't feel like I'm being watched or anything like that, other than just the normal customer looking at me while I work. It just seems to happen randomly while I'm at the register.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you ever lose time or have any memory gap for what happened?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I remember everything that happens. I'm completely aware that I'm tapping the scanner or just... standing there frozen. I can see the customer waiting, I know I should be moving, but I just can't stop what I'm doing or get myself unstuck. So, no, I don't lose any time or forget what happened. I'm awake and aware for the whole thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have sudden, brief movements like jerking, blinking, shoulder shrugging, or other repeated motions that happen without you meaning to?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have anything like that. Um, no jerking or extra blinking or anything. It's really just what I mentioned before... the tapping the scanner or just completely freezing in place. I don't get those sudden twitches or shrugs you're talking about.\n\nDoctor: Do these tapping or freezing episodes ever happen while you’re asleep, or only when you’re awake?\n\nPatient: No, they only happen when I'm awake. I mean, as far as I know anyway. I've never woken up tapping something, and I sleep pretty normally. It's really just when I'm at work, at the register, completely awake.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you ever feel like you blank out or lose awareness of what you’re doing?\n\nPatient: No, I don't blank out. Like I said before, I'm fully aware of everything going on around me. I know I'm stuck or tapping, I just... I can't stop it. But my mind is completely clear and I know exactly what I'm doing the whole time.\n\nDoctor: When these episodes start and stop, do they come on and end suddenly?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they do. It's... it's just like a switch flips. One second I'm scanning a barcode just fine, and the next second I'm just stuck there, or my hand is tapping the machine. And then, just as fast, it stops and I can move normally again. I just know I'm stuck there, and I can't really tell you exactly how much I'm taking in while it's happening.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you feel like you’re in a trance or disconnected from what’s happening around you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I'd call it a trance. I don't really feel disconnected from myself or anything like that. \n\nI mean, I know I'm standing right there at the register. I know the customer is looking at me. My mind hasn't gone anywhere else... I'm just stuck. It's more like my body just stops working right, not like I'm zoned out or floating away.\n\nDoctor: When the tapping happens, is it always the same exact pattern each time?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't paid that close attention to the actual tapping itself. I just know my hand is hitting the scanner... but whether it's the exact same rhythm or, you know, the same number of taps every single time, I couldn't tell you. I'm usually just focused on trying to get it to stop so I can finish ringing up the customer.\n\nDoctor: When this happens, is the movement or freezing usually the same each time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's pretty much those same things every time. I just keep getting stuck doing things at the register that really don't fit the moment. \n\nIt's either tapping the scanner like I mentioned, or... or I'll just completely freeze with my hand hovering right over the open cash drawer while the customer is just standing there waiting for their change. It's actually the main reason I came in to see you. Last week it finally got me written up at work, so I really need to figure out how to make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes tend to happen in the same situations each time, like when you’re stressed or being watched?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed a specific trigger, to be honest. I mean, ringing up customers can be a little stressful sometimes, especially when there's a long line, but it doesn't only happen then. And I don't feel like I'm being watched or anything like that, other than just the normal customer looking at me while I work. It just seems to happen randomly while I'm at the register.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you ever lose time or have any memory gap for what happened?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I remember everything that happens. I'm completely aware that I'm tapping the scanner or just... standing there frozen. I can see the customer waiting, I know I should be moving, but I just can't stop what I'm doing or get myself unstuck. So, no, I don't lose any time or forget what happened. I'm awake and aware for the whole thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have sudden, brief movements like jerking, blinking, shoulder shrugging, or other repeated motions that happen without you meaning to?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have anything like that. Um, no jerking or extra blinking or anything. It's really just what I mentioned before... the tapping the scanner or just completely freezing in place. I don't get those sudden twitches or shrugs you're talking about.\n\nDoctor: Do these tapping or freezing episodes ever happen while you’re asleep, or only when you’re awake?\n\nPatient: No, they only happen when I'm awake. I mean, as far as I know anyway. I've never woken up tapping something, and I sleep pretty normally. It's really just when I'm at work, at the register, completely awake.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you ever feel like you blank out or lose awareness of what you’re doing?\n\nPatient: No, I don't blank out. Like I said before, I'm fully aware of everything going on around me. I know I'm stuck or tapping, I just... I can't stop it. But my mind is completely clear and I know exactly what I'm doing the whole time.\n\nDoctor: When these episodes start and stop, do they come on and end suddenly?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they do. It's... it's just like a switch flips. One second I'm scanning a barcode just fine, and the next second I'm just stuck there, or my hand is tapping the machine. And then, just as fast, it stops and I can move normally again. There's no build-up or anything, it just happens.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes ever start when you’re feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed a connection like that. I don't think it has anything to do with being stressed or upset. I could be having a perfectly fine day, just a normal, quiet shift at the register, and it'll still happen. It doesn't really seem to matter how I'm feeling at the time, it just comes on out of nowhere.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF44.4, F98.4\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm here because I've been having some trouble at work. I'm a cashier, and lately at the register, I just... I keep getting stuck doing things that don't really fit the moment. \n\nLike, I'll catch myself tapping the scanner in this specific pattern, or I'll just freeze up completely with my hand hovering right over the cash drawer while a customer is standing there waiting for their change. It's becoming a real problem. Last week, it actually got me written up by my manager, so... I figured I needed to come in and talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an urge or sense of tension right before you do those movements, with relief afterward?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't really say it's like that. I don't feel an urge or any tension before it happens. And there's no relief afterwards, either. \n\nIt's more like... I just realize I'm doing it, or I just find myself completely stuck. It's not something I'm choosing to do to feel better, it just sort of happens.\n\nDoctor: Can you hold back or suppress the movements if you try?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, like I was saying before about getting stuck at the register... I can't just snap out of it. If I could stop myself from freezing over the cash drawer, or stop tapping the scanner, I definitely would. Especially with a customer right there waiting. But I just... I can't seem to hold it back or control it. That's exactly why I ended up getting that write-up last week.\n\nDoctor: When this happens and you’re frozen or doing the tapping, are you still fully aware of what’s going on around you?\n\nPatient: I'm... I'm not entirely sure. I mean, I know I'm at work, and I know there's a customer standing right in front of me waiting. But as far as being fully aware of everything else going on around me in the store... I haven't really noticed. \n\nIt's hard to say, honestly. I just know I'm stuck there, and I can't really tell you exactly how much I'm taking in while it's happening.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you feel like you’re in a trance or disconnected from what’s happening around you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I'd call it a trance. I don't really feel disconnected from myself or anything like that. \n\nI mean, I know I'm standing right there at the register. I know the customer is looking at me. My mind hasn't gone anywhere else... I'm just stuck. It's more like my body just stops working right, not like I'm zoned out or floating away.\n\nDoctor: When the tapping happens, is it always the same exact pattern each time?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't paid that close attention to the actual tapping itself. I just know my hand is hitting the scanner... but whether it's the exact same rhythm or, you know, the same number of taps every single time, I couldn't tell you. I'm usually just focused on trying to get it to stop so I can finish ringing up the customer.\n\nDoctor: When this happens, is the movement or freezing usually the same each time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's pretty much those same things every time. I just keep getting stuck doing things at the register that really don't fit the moment. \n\nIt's either tapping the scanner like I mentioned, or... or I'll just completely freeze with my hand hovering right over the open cash drawer while the customer is just standing there waiting for their change. It's actually the main reason I came in to see you. Last week it finally got me written up at work, so I really need to figure out how to make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes tend to happen in the same situations each time, like when you’re stressed or being watched?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed a specific trigger, to be honest. I mean, ringing up customers can be a little stressful sometimes, especially when there's a long line, but it doesn't only happen then. And I don't feel like I'm being watched or anything like that, other than just the normal customer looking at me while I work. It just seems to happen randomly while I'm at the register.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you ever lose time or have any memory gap for what happened?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I remember everything that happens. I'm completely aware that I'm tapping the scanner or just... standing there frozen. I can see the customer waiting, I know I should be moving, but I just can't stop what I'm doing or get myself unstuck. So, no, I don't lose any time or forget what happened. I'm awake and aware for the whole thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have sudden, brief movements like jerking, blinking, shoulder shrugging, or other repeated motions that happen without you meaning to?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have anything like that. Um, no jerking or extra blinking or anything. It's really just what I mentioned before... the tapping the scanner or just completely freezing in place. I don't get those sudden twitches or shrugs you're talking about.\n\nDoctor: Do these tapping or freezing episodes ever happen while you’re asleep, or only when you’re awake?\n\nPatient: No, they only happen when I'm awake. I mean, as far as I know anyway. I've never woken up tapping something, and I sleep pretty normally. It's really just when I'm at work, at the register, completely awake.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you ever feel like you blank out or lose awareness of what you’re doing?\n\nPatient: No, I don't blank out. Like I said before, I'm fully aware of everything going on around me. I know I'm stuck or tapping, I just... I can't stop it. But my mind is completely clear and I know exactly what I'm doing the whole time.\n\nDoctor: When these episodes start and stop, do they come on and end suddenly?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they do. It's... it's just like a switch flips. One second I'm scanning a barcode just fine, and the next second I'm just stuck there, or my hand is tapping the machine. And then, just as fast, it stops and I can move normally again. There's no build-up or anything, it just happens.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes ever start when you’re feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed a connection like that. I don't think it has anything to do with being stressed or upset. I could be having a perfectly fine day, just a normal, quiet shift at the register, and it'll still happen. It doesn't really seem to matter how I'm feeling at the time, it just comes on out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: When one of these episodes happens, do you feel any inner urge or build-up right before it starts?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. There's no... feeling like I need to do it, or like a pressure building up. Like I was saying a minute ago, it really just happens out of nowhere. I don't get any warning or urge beforehand, my body just starts doing it.\n\nDoctor: How old were you when these episodes first started?\n\nPatient: I was... well, I'm 54 now. It started about two years and four months ago. I remember because it was right around the time my youngest moved away to the Eastern Shore for college. It's just been building up since then.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that these episodes seem to happen more when there’s a clear benefit for you, like getting out of work or avoiding a task?\n\nPatient: No, no one has ever said anything like that to me. And honestly, it doesn't get me out of anything anyway. Like I said, it happens even when I'm just standing at the register doing my job. There's no... no benefit to it, it just happens when it happens.\n\nDoctor: When the tapping starts, does it feel like something you do on purpose, or does it happen completely without any intention on your part?\n\nPatient: No, it's definitely not on purpose. I don't... I don't mean to do it at all. It just happens completely on its own, without me wanting to or trying to. It's like my body just decides to do it.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you ever hold unusual postures or positions for a long time, even when it would normally be uncomfortable?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't noticed getting stuck in any weird positions or anything like that. It's really just the tapping that I mentioned. I can move the rest of my body around just fine... so, no, I don't hold any uncomfortable postures.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I'm not entirely sure. I mean, I know I'm at work, and I know there's a customer standing right in front of me waiting. But as far as being fully aware of everything else going on around me in the store... I haven't really noticed. \n\nIt's hard to say, honestly. I just know I'm stuck there, and I can't really tell you exactly how much I'm taking in while it's happening.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you feel like you’re in a trance or disconnected from what’s happening around you?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I'd call it a trance. I don't really feel disconnected from myself or anything like that. \n\nI mean, I know I'm standing right there at the register. I know the customer is looking at me. My mind hasn't gone anywhere else... I'm just stuck. It's more like my body just stops working right, not like I'm zoned out or floating away.\n\nDoctor: When the tapping happens, is it always the same exact pattern each time?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't paid that close attention to the actual tapping itself. I just know my hand is hitting the scanner... but whether it's the exact same rhythm or, you know, the same number of taps every single time, I couldn't tell you. I'm usually just focused on trying to get it to stop so I can finish ringing up the customer.\n\nDoctor: When this happens, is the movement or freezing usually the same each time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's pretty much those same things every time. I just keep getting stuck doing things at the register that really don't fit the moment. \n\nIt's either tapping the scanner like I mentioned, or... or I'll just completely freeze with my hand hovering right over the open cash drawer while the customer is just standing there waiting for their change. It's actually the main reason I came in to see you. Last week it finally got me written up at work, so I really need to figure out how to make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes tend to happen in the same situations each time, like when you’re stressed or being watched?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed a specific trigger, to be honest. I mean, ringing up customers can be a little stressful sometimes, especially when there's a long line, but it doesn't only happen then. And I don't feel like I'm being watched or anything like that, other than just the normal customer looking at me while I work. It just seems to happen randomly while I'm at the register.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you ever lose time or have any memory gap for what happened?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I remember everything that happens. I'm completely aware that I'm tapping the scanner or just... standing there frozen. I can see the customer waiting, I know I should be moving, but I just can't stop what I'm doing or get myself unstuck. So, no, I don't lose any time or forget what happened. I'm awake and aware for the whole thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have sudden, brief movements like jerking, blinking, shoulder shrugging, or other repeated motions that happen without you meaning to?\n\nPatient: No, I don't have anything like that. Um, no jerking or extra blinking or anything. It's really just what I mentioned before... the tapping the scanner or just completely freezing in place. I don't get those sudden twitches or shrugs you're talking about.\n\nDoctor: Do these tapping or freezing episodes ever happen while you’re asleep, or only when you’re awake?\n\nPatient: No, they only happen when I'm awake. I mean, as far as I know anyway. I've never woken up tapping something, and I sleep pretty normally. It's really just when I'm at work, at the register, completely awake.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you ever feel like you blank out or lose awareness of what you’re doing?\n\nPatient: No, I don't blank out. Like I said before, I'm fully aware of everything going on around me. I know I'm stuck or tapping, I just... I can't stop it. But my mind is completely clear and I know exactly what I'm doing the whole time.\n\nDoctor: When these episodes start and stop, do they come on and end suddenly?\n\nPatient: Yeah, they do. It's... it's just like a switch flips. One second I'm scanning a barcode just fine, and the next second I'm just stuck there, or my hand is tapping the machine. And then, just as fast, it stops and I can move normally again. There's no build-up or anything, it just happens.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes ever start when you’re feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed a connection like that. I don't think it has anything to do with being stressed or upset. I could be having a perfectly fine day, just a normal, quiet shift at the register, and it'll still happen. It doesn't really seem to matter how I'm feeling at the time, it just comes on out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: When one of these episodes happens, do you feel any inner urge or build-up right before it starts?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. There's no... feeling like I need to do it, or like a pressure building up. Like I was saying a minute ago, it really just happens out of nowhere. I don't get any warning or urge beforehand, my body just starts doing it.\n\nDoctor: How old were you when these episodes first started?\n\nPatient: I was... well, I'm 54 now. It started about two years and four months ago. I remember because it was right around the time my youngest moved away to the Eastern Shore for college. It's just been building up since then.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone told you that these episodes seem to happen more when there’s a clear benefit for you, like getting out of work or avoiding a task?\n\nPatient: No, no one has ever said anything like that to me. And honestly, it doesn't get me out of anything anyway. Like I said, it happens even when I'm just standing at the register doing my job. There's no... no benefit to it, it just happens when it happens.\n\nDoctor: When the tapping starts, does it feel like something you do on purpose, or does it happen completely without any intention on your part?\n\nPatient: No, it's definitely not on purpose. I don't... I don't mean to do it at all. It just happens completely on its own, without me wanting to or trying to. It's like my body just decides to do it.\n\nDoctor: During these episodes, do you ever hold unusual postures or positions for a long time, even when it would normally be uncomfortable?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't noticed getting stuck in any weird positions or anything like that. It's really just the tapping that I mentioned. I can move the rest of my body around just fine... so, no, I don't hold any uncomfortable postures.\n\nDoctor: When you get stuck at the register, do you stay frozen or in that same posture for a long time before it passes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't stay frozen. It's just... I just stand there like I normally would, doing my job, but my hand is just tapping. I'm not stuck like a statue or anything, I just can't get my hand to stop for a minute or two.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
